Initially energized by the creative freedom, she started taking on too many projects at once, staying up most nights working on intricate mixed‑media pieces and planning an immersive exhibition that blended her art with elaborate sound and light elements. Over roughly the next year, friends and collaborators noticed her becoming increasingly intense and hard to reach between bursts of productivity, missing meetings or appearing preoccupied and guarded during studio visits, which led to frayed professional relationships and a stalled major exhibit. When she began canceling hikes and yoga classes she once relied on for balance and confided to a close friend that her mind felt like it was “running too fast to keep up with,” the friend helped her arrange an appointment with a community mental health clinic attached to an arts nonprofit.\n- Chief Complaint: “My thoughts won’t slow down—they’re racing all the time and I can’t turn them off long enough to focus or rest.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): For months now I’ve been convinced that the people who run the big galleries here are tracking me online and sabotaging my freelance gigs because they’re threatened by what I’m doing, even when my friends show me there’s no sign of it in the emails or contracts.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Pretty regularly, especially late at night in my studio, I hear this male voice just over my shoulder commenting on my brushstrokes or telling me I’m “chosen” or “a fraud,” even though there’s nobody there and the building is quiet.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I’m talking lately, I’ll start out trying to explain a project and then my words spin off into color theory, then Vegas neon history, then some random memory from high school, and I can see people’s faces go blank because they can’t follow where I’m going.\n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): Sometimes I’ll pace around my apartment for an hour straight, waving my arms and talking to myself about this huge immersive show I’m planning, then suddenly just freeze in a weird pose staring at a spot on the wall until I realize how strange I must look.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing between feeling ridiculously amped up and invincible about my art—like I’m on the verge of changing the whole Vegas scene—to snapping at my roommate over something tiny, like moving my paintbrushes, and then feeling embarrassed but still kind of wired.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I get these stretches where I’m bouncing from rearranging my studio to sketching new concepts to emailing venues at 3 a.m., and even when my body is tired I just keep going because it feels impossible to sit still or do nothing.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): During these high periods I honestly feel like I’m some kind of visionary, like I’m going to be the one who puts Las Vegas on the global art map, and regular advice from curators or friends just seems beneath what I’m capable of.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On my intense weeks I’ll sleep maybe three hours a night and wake up feeling wired and ready to dive back into my projects, and I don’t even miss the sleep until I crash days later.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My brain runs like a pinball machine, jumping from a new installation idea to worrying about rent to remembering some random comment from a stranger, all overlapping so fast that I can’t hold onto one thought long enough to finish a painting or even follow a full conversation.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll sit down to work on a commission and within minutes I’m pulled away by the flicker of a light, a car horn outside, a text notification, or even the way the shadows move on the wall, and before I know it I’ve started five different things and finished none of them.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I was at my most wired this past spring—sleeping just a few hours, emailing galleries all night, and feeling unstoppable—that same voice was constantly commenting on my paintings and I was sure the big galleries were watching and blocking my shows.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Ever since I left my steady gallery job, most of this time I’ve been in some version of that revved‑up, overconfident, can’t‑slow‑down mode, with only short stretches where I feel closer to my old baseline.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: Even during a quieter spell a couple of months ago when my mood felt more flat and I wasn’t buzzing with energy, I still heard the same male voice in the studio and stayed convinced the gallery people were secretly tracking and undermining me.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve missed deadlines, lost a big exhibit opportunity, pulled away from friends, and let basic things like laundry, cleaning, and regular meals slide to the point my roommate has had to step in.\n- duration: All of this—the racing mind, mood swings, and strange experiences—has been building and sticking around for a little over a year since I switched to full‑time freelance and started curating my own shows.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The feeling that galleries are sabotaging me and the voice in my studio have been showing up regularly for most of this past year, not just during the worst weeks.\n- manic_episode_duration: These long, amped‑up stretches where I barely sleep, feel invincible about my art, and can’t slow my thoughts have been happening on and off for most of the past year since going freelance.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English enough to focus or rest.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): For months now I’ve been convinced that the people who run the big galleries here are tracking me online and sabotaging my freelance gigs because they’re threatened by what I’m doing, even when my friends show me there’s no sign of it in the emails or contracts.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Pretty regularly, especially late at night in my studio, I hear this male voice just over my shoulder commenting on my brushstrokes or telling me I’m “chosen” or “a fraud,” even though there’s nobody there and the building is quiet.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I’m talking lately, I’ll start out trying to explain a project and then my words spin off into color theory, then Vegas neon history, then some random memory from high school, and I can see people’s faces go blank because they can’t follow where I’m going.\n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): Sometimes I’ll pace around my realize how strange I must look.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing between feeling ridiculously amped up and invincible about my art—like I’m on the verge of changing the whole Vegas scene—to snapping at my roommate over something tiny, like moving my paintbrushes, and then feeling embarrassed but still kind of wired.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I get these stretches where I’m bouncing from rearranging my studio to sketching new concepts to emailing venues at 3 a.m., and even when my body is tired I just keep going because it feels impossible to sit still or do nothing.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): During these high periods I honestly feel like I’m some kind of visionary, like I’m going to be the one who puts Las Vegas on the global art map, and regular advice from curators or friends just seems beneath what I’m capable of.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On my intense weeks I’ll sleep maybe three hours a night and wake up feeling wired and ready to dive back into my projects, and I don’t even miss the sleep until I crash days later.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My brain runs like a pinball machine, jumping from a new installation idea to worrying about rent to remembering some random comment from a stranger, all overlapping so fast that I can’t hold onto one thought long enough to finish a painting or even follow a full conversation.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll sit down to work on a commission and within minutes I’m pulled away by the flicker of a light, a car horn outside, a text notification, or even the way the shadows move on the wall, and before I know it I’ve started five different things and finished none of them.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: When I gallery job, most of this time I’ve been in some version of that revved‑up, overconfident, can’t‑slow‑down mode, with only short stretches where I feel closer to my old baseline.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: Even during a quieter spell a couple of months ago when my mood felt more flat and I wasn’t buzzing with energy, I still heard the same male voice in the studio and stayed convinced the gallery people were secretly tracking and undermining me.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve missed deadlines, lost a big exhibit opportunity, pulled away from friends, and let basic things like laundry, cleaning, and regular meals slide to the point my roommate has had to step in.\n- duration: All of this—the racing mind, mood swings, and strange experiences—has been building and sticking around for a little over a year since I switched to full‑time freelance and started curating my own Initially energized by the creative freedom, she started taking on too many projects at once, staying up most nights working on intricate mixed‑media pieces and planning an immersive exhibition that blended her art with elaborate sound and light elements. Over roughly the next year, friends and collaborators noticed her becoming increasingly intense and hard to reach between bursts of productivity, missing meetings or appearing preoccupied and guarded during studio visits, which led to frayed professional relationships and a stalled major exhibit. When she began canceling hikes and yoga classes she once relied on for balance and confided to a close friend that her mind felt like it was “running too fast to keep up with,” the friend helped her especially late at night in my studio, I hear this male voice just over my shoulder commenting on my brushstrokes or telling me I’m “chosen” or “a fraud,” even though there’s nobody there and the building is quiet.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I’m talking lately, I’ll start out trying to explain a project and then my words spin off into color theory, then Vegas neon history, curators or friends just seems beneath what I’m capable of.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On my intense weeks I’ll sleep maybe three hours a night and wake up feeling wired and ready to dive back into my projects, and I don’t even miss the sleep until I crash days later.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My brain runs like a pinball machine, jumping from a new installation idea to worrying about rent to remembering some random comment from a stranger, all overlapping so fast that I can’t hold onto one thought long enough to finish a painting or even follow a full conversation.\n- S039 snapping at my roommate over something tiny, like moving my paintbrushes, and then feeling embarrassed but still kind of wired.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I get these stretches where I’m bouncing from rearranging my studio to sketching new concepts to emailing venues at 3 a.m., and even when my body is tired I just keep going because it feels impossible to sit still or do nothing.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): During these high periods I honestly feel like I’m some kind of visionary, like I’m going to be the one who puts Las Vegas on the global art map, and regular advice from curators or friends just seems beneath what I’m capable of.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On my intense weeks I’ll sleep maybe three hours a night and wake up feeling wired and ready to dive back into my projects, and I don’t even miss the sleep until I crash days later.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My brain runs like a pinball machine, jumping from a new installation idea to worrying about rent to remembering some random comment from a stranger, all overlapping so fast that I can’t hold onto one thought long enough to finish a painting or even follow a full conversation.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll sit down to work on a commission and within minutes I’m pulled away by the flicker of a light, a car horn outside, a text notification, or even the way the shadows move galleries were watching and blocking my shows.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Ever since I left my steady gallery job, most of this time I’ve been in some version of that revved‑up, overconfident, can’t‑slow‑down mode, with only short stretches where I feel closer to my old baseline.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: Even during a quieter spell a couple of months ago when my mood felt more flat and I wasn’t buzzing with energy, I still heard the same male voice in the studio and stayed convinced the gallery people were secretly tracking and undermining me.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve missed deadlines, lost a big exhibit opportunity, pulled away from friends, and let basic things like laundry, cleaning, and regular meals slide to the point my roommate has had to step in.\n- duration: All of this—the racing mind, mood swings, and strange experiences—has been building and sticking around for a little over a year since I switched to full‑time freelance and started curating my own Initially energized by the creative freedom, she started taking on too many projects at once, staying up most nights working on intricate mixed‑media pieces and planning an immersive exhibition that blended her art with elaborate sound and light elements. Over roughly the next year, friends and collaborators noticed her becoming increasingly intense and hard to reach between bursts of productivity, missing meetings or appearing preoccupied and guarded during studio visits, which led to frayed professional relationships and a stalled major exhibit. When she began canceling hikes and yoga classes she once relied on for balance and confided to a close friend that her mind felt like it was “running too fast to keep up with,” the friend helped her remembering some random comment from a stranger, all overlapping so fast that I can’t hold onto one thought long enough to finish a painting or even follow a full conversation.\n- S039 It feels like my mind is running too fast to keep up with, and I just can't find a way to quiet it down.\n\nDoctor: Are these racing thoughts mostly uncontrollable worries about everyday life, or do they feel more like a rapid flow of different ideas, perhaps accompanied by high energy?\n\nPatient: It’s definitely more of a rapid flow of creative ideas and plans, like my brain is a pinball machine jumping from one thought to another. It comes in these intense, distinct stretches of a few days or weeks where I feel completely amped up and barely sleep, rather than being a constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods in your life where you felt the opposite—very down, empty, or completely lost interest in things for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced prolonged periods of feeling deeply down, empty, or losing interest in things. I've had these intense, high-energy periods on and off for about a year now, ever since I transitioned to full-time freelance work. I only get short, temporary stretches in between where I feel closer to my normal baseline before the buzzing energy starts right back up again.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, have you ever made impulsive decisions, spent excessive money, or taken risks that caused problems in your work or relationships?"

It comes in these intense, distinct stretches of a few days or weeks where I feel completely amped up and barely sleep, rather than being a constant thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods in your life where you felt the opposite—very down, empty, or completely lost interest in things for weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced prolonged periods of feeling deeply down, empty, or losing interest in things. Sometimes I'll feel really flat or physically exhausted and crashed after one of my high-energy stretches, but it doesn't feel like a deep, lasting depression.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced multiple separate periods of this high energy with times of feeling back to your normal self in between, or is this current stretch the very first time it has happened?\n\nPatient: No, this isn't the first time at all. I've had these intense, high-energy periods on and off for about a year now, ever since I transitioned to full-time freelance work. I only get short, temporary stretches in between where I feel closer to my normal baseline before the buzzing energy starts right back up again.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, have you ever made impulsive decisions, spent excessive money, or taken risks that caused problems in your work or relationships?\n\nPatient: I haven't really spent excessive money or taken major physical risks, though I have impulsively taken on too many projects at once and emailed venues at 3 a.m. without thinking it through. That impulsiveness has definitely strained some of my professional relationships and caused me to miss important deadlines.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, have they ever been so intense that you needed hospital care, or did family and friends become extremely concerned for your safety?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never been hospitalized or needed hospital care. But my roommate and friends got really concerned because I've been pulling away and letting basic things like chores and eating slide, to the point where my roommate had to step in and help. It was actually a close friend who noticed how intense my behavior was getting and helped me set up this appointment.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, are you using any recreational drugs, stimulants, or high amounts of caffeine, or do you have any medical conditions like thyroid issues?"
  }

No, I don't use any recreational drugs, stimulants, or even much caffeine, and I don't have any medical conditions like thyroid issues. All of this is just happening on its own.

But my roommate and friends got really concerned because I've been pulling away and letting basic things like chores and eating slide, to the point where my roommate had to step in and help. It was actually a close friend who noticed how intense my behavior was getting and helped me set up this appointment.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, are you using any recreational drugs, stimulants, or high amounts of caffeine, or do you have any medical conditions like thyroid issues?\n\nPatient: No, I don't use any recreational drugs, stimulants, or even much caffeine, and I don't have any medical conditions like thyroid issues. All of this is just happening on its own.\n\nDoctor: When you have these high-energy periods, how long do they typically last—do they usually last for just a few days, or do they consistently go on for a full week or longer?"
  }
]

They consistently last for a week or longer, often stretching into several weeks at a time. During these periods, I can go for weeks sleeping only about three hours a night and feeling completely wired, with only short breaks before I crash or temporarily return to my normal baseline.

Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.
